```
async def retrieve(queries: RetrievalQueries) -> list[RetrievedChunk]:
    """Search every branch and return the fused top chunks."""
    settings = get_settings()
    texts = queries.texts()

    # One batched forward pass for every branch, rather than one call each.
    # Embedding is CPU-bound and synchronous, so it goes off the event loop.
    embeddings = await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(embed_documents, texts)

                collection_name=settings.qdrant_collection,
                prefetch=_prefetch(embeddings, settings.retrieval_prefetch_limit),
                query=models.FusionQuery(fusion=models.Fusion.RRF),
                limit=settings.retrieval_top_k,
                with_payload=True,
            )
        )
    except Exception as error:
        # Degrade, don't fail: the generator will say it cannot answer.
        logger.error(
            "retrieval failed, answering without context: %s: %s",
            type(error).__name__,
            error,
        )
        return []

    chunks = [RetrievedChunk.from_point(point) for point in response.points]
    logger.debug(

=== embedding: model config and batching ===
def get_embedder() -> BGEM3FlagModel:
    """Loaded once per process — several GB of weights."""
    settings = get_settings()
    logger.info("loading embedding model %s", settings.embedding_model)
    model = BGEM3FlagModel(
        settings.embedding_model,
        # fp16 is a GPU optimisation; on CPU it is slower and less accurate.
        use_fp16=False,
        # Normalised vectors let Qdrant's cosine distance work as a dot product.
        normalize_embeddings=True,
        # FlagEmbedding truncates at 512 by default, which would silently cut
        # the chunks whose heading path pushes them past the chunk budget — the
        # tail would be embedded as if it did not exist. BGE-M3 itself accepts
        # 8192; this only needs to clear the longest contextualised chunk.
        passage_max_length=settings.embed_max_tokens,
        query_max_length=settings.embed_max_tokens,
    )
    logger.info("embedding model ready")
    return model


def _to_embeddings(output) -> list[Embedding]:
```

Here's the assessment.

**What the item describes is real.** `retriever.py:136` embeds off the event loop through anyio's default thread pool. That pool allows **40** concurrent threads, and each BGE-M3 forward pass asks torch for **6** threads — so in the worst case 240 threads compete for 12 cores. CPU oversubscription degrades superlinearly, so past a point every request slows and throughput doesn't improve.

One correction to the item's framing: it says "nothing caps" the concurrency. Something does — the cap is 40. It's just far too high for CPU-bound work.

**But the arithmetic says it won't bite.** Concurrency in a stage is arrival rate × time in that stage, so concurrent embeds relative to concurrent requests is just the duration ratio: **33 ms of a ~3,200 ms request ≈ 1/97**.

| concurrent requests | concurrent embeds |
|---|---|
| 100 | ~1 |
| 200 | ~2 — contention begins |
| **3,900** | **40 — the limit is reached** |

You'd need roughly four thousand simultaneous requests to saturate what's already there. And at 200 concurrent you're making ~400 concurrent Anthropic calls, so rate limits and the 1.3 s generation stage fill the queue long before the embedder does.

**So: keep it, drop its priority.** It's ~5 lines and removes a failure class — cheap insurance, not wasted work — but it isn't a latency fix for anything measurable today, and the item currently reads like one.

Two things worth correcting regardless:

- **A dedicated limiter, never a smaller global one.** The same thread pool serves the Qdrant query at `retriever.py:139`, which is I/O-bound and *wants* concurrency. Shrinking anyio's default would throttle search too. The right shape is a module-level `anyio.CapacityLimiter(2)` in `common/embedding.py` passed only to the embed call.
- **Measure before bounding.** `work.md` → Testing already has the concurrent-users test. That's what would reveal embed contention, and it's what turns this from assumed to observed — including the possibility that p95 doesn't move at all at N=20, in which case the honest outcome is closing the item as "measured, not a problem" rather than implementing it.

Your premise is right, and it's already the documented decision — but it points the other way from what the question implies. `docs/considerations.md:51-56` says:

> The alternative is an **embedder service**… BGE-M3 stored once instead of twice, the api back to 507 MB with a fast rebuild, and the model scaled — or GPU-placed — independently of a request path that is otherwise IO-bound. It is the better production shape and it was not built here: a third service and an HTTP contract buy nothing on one machine.

**Splitting doesn't address the bottleneck.** The api saturates first, but on *Anthropic calls* — 2 per request, ~2.5 s of the ~3.2 s. Moving embedding (1%, 33 ms) to another box doesn't shorten that queue; it adds a network hop to the one part that was never the problem. As a throughput fix it's close to a no-op.

**What splitting actually buys is deployment economics**, and those are real: 507 MB vs 16.4 GB per api image, the model stored once instead of once per replica, ~8 min build time gone from the api's iteration loop, and the option to GPU-place the embedder independently.

**Which gives a concrete trigger: more than one api replica.** Today one container carries a 2.3 GB model doing 1% of the work — mildly wasteful. At N replicas you pay N × 2.3 GB of RAM and N × 16.4 GB of image pull for that same 1%, while the thing you actually need to scale is an I/O-bound request path. That's when the economics invert — driven by replica count, not by load.

One corollary worth noting: **splitting relocates the semaphore question rather than removing it.** A dedicated embedder service still needs a concurrency bound — but there it's natural (a CPU-bound service sized to cores ÷ torch threads) instead of a subtlety buried in a request handler. The two open items are linked, and the embedder-service move would absorb the semaphore one.

Tables get no special treatment — Docling flattens them to text inside a normal chunk, and that text is what's embedded and what reaches the model.

The serialisation is the interesting part. Here's the actual indexed chunk for the returns table (`aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx`, chunk 4):

```
Consumer, **Return window** = 30 days. Consumer, **Condition** = Unopened. Consumer, **Restocking fee** = None. …
Business, **Return window** = 14 days. Business, **Condition** = Unopened. Business, **Restocking fee** = None. …
Business, **Return window** = 14 days. Business, **Condition** = Opened, complete. Business, **Restocking fee** = 15%. …
```

`HybridChunker` emits one `<first column>, <header> = <value>` triplet per cell, in row-major order, as one run-on paragraph. Three consequences:

- **Rows have no boundary.** Nothing separates the end of one row from the start of the next — not a newline, not a marker. Row identity is carried only by position in the stream.
- **The key is the first column only.** Every triplet is keyed `Business,` — the discriminating column here is `Condition`, which appears as just another value. So "Business, Restocking fee = None" (unopened) and "Business, Restocking fee = 15%" (opened, complete) are textually near-identical and adjacent.
- That is precisely the qa-001 failure. The model answered "no restocking fee" because the unopened row's `None` sits one clause before the opened-complete row's `15%`, with no boundary between them.

Other mechanics: the whole table landed in one chunk merged with the prose before and after it plus the caption line, so nothing was split — `repeat_table_header` and `merge_peers` are on but didn't need to fire. Embedding is the same single BGE-M3 pass as any chunk, over `contextualize()` output (heading path prepended); `text` — the flat triplet stream above — is what goes to the LLM.

So the work.md watch item's proposed fix ("keeping table rows addressable through chunking") means changing this serialisation: one row per line, keyed by the columns that actually discriminate. That's a Docling serialiser override at chunk time, not a retrieval change.

```
class ChunkingDocSerializer(MarkdownDocSerializer):
    """Doc serializer used for chunking purposes."""

    table_serializer: BaseTableSerializer = TripletTableSerializer()
    params: MarkdownParams = MarkdownParams(
        image_mode=ImageRefMode.PLACEHOLDER,
        image_placeholder="",
        escape_underscores=False,
        escape_html=False,
    )


class ChunkingSerializerProvider(BaseSerializerProvider):
    """Serializer provider used for chunking purposes."""

    @override
    def get_serializer(self, doc: DoclingDocument) -> BaseDocSerializer:
        """Get the associated serializer."""
        return ChunkingDocSerializer(doc=doc)
```

```
    def segment(self, doc_chunk: DocChunk, available_length: int, doc_serializer: BaseDocSerializer) -> list[str]:
        """Split a single doc chunk into a list of text segments.

        Two strategies are applied depending on the chunk's content:

        - Table with header repetition: when ``repeat_table_header`` is
          enabled and the chunk contains exactly one ``TableItem``, the table is
          split line-by-line via ``LineBasedTokenChunker``.  The header rows are
          used as a prefix so they are reproduced at the top of every segment.
          Any preamble text that precedes the first table row (e.g. a caption) is
          included in the prefix to account for its token cost, but is stripped
          from all segments after the first so it does not repeat.

        - Semantic splitting: for all other chunks, ``semchunk`` splits the
          text at natural semantic boundaries up to ``available_length`` tokens.

        Args:
            doc_chunk: The chunk to segment.
            available_length: Maximum token budget for the semantic-splitting
                path (ignored for the table path, which uses ``self.tokenizer``
                and ``self.max_tokens`` internally).
            doc_serializer: Serializer for the current document; must be a
                ``ChunkingDocSerializer`` for the table path to activate.

        Returns:
            A list of text segments derived from ``doc_chunk.text``.
        """
        segments = []
        if (
            self.repeat_table_header
            and isinstance(doc_serializer, ChunkingDocSerializer)
            and len(doc_chunk.meta.doc_items) == 1
            and isinstance(doc_chunk.meta.doc_items[0], TableItem)
        ):
            header_lines, body_lines = doc_serializer.table_serializer.get_header_and_body_lines(
                table_text=doc_chunk.text
            )

            if header_lines:
                header_start = doc_chunk.text.find(header_lines[0])
                preamble = doc_chunk.text[:header_start] if header_start > 0 else ""
            else:
                preamble = ""
            table_prefix = "".join(header_lines)
            full_prefix = preamble + table_prefix

            line_chunker = LineBasedTokenChunker(
                tokenizer=self.tokenizer,
                prefix=full_prefix,
                omit_prefix_on_overflow=self.omit_header_on_overflow,
                serializer_provider=self.serializer_provider,
            )
            segments = line_chunker.chunk_text(lines=body_lines)
            if preamble:
                segments = segments[:1] + [s[len(preamble) :] for s in segments[1:]]
        else:
            if not _SEMCHUNK_AVAILABLE:
                raise ImportError(_SEMCHUNK_INSTALL_HINT) from _SEMCHUNK_IMPORT_ERROR
            sem_chunker = semchunk.chunkerify(self.tokenizer.get_tokenizer(), chunk_size=available_length)
            sem_segments = sem_chunker(doc_chunk.text)
            segments = cast(list[str], sem_segments)
        return segments

    def _merge_chunks_with_matching_metadata(self, chunks: list[DocChunk]):
        output_chunks = []
        window_start = 0
        window_end = 0  # an inclusive index
        num_chunks = len(chunks)
        while window_end < num_chunks:
            chunk = chunks[window_end]
```

```
def _pdf_options() -> PdfPipelineOptions:
    options = PdfPipelineOptions()
    # OCR reads text rendered *inside* images — chart axis labels, legends,
    # numbers in diagrams — which the captioner does not reliably transcribe.
    options.do_ocr = True
    options.do_table_structure = True
    # Without this Docling discards figure images after layout analysis, and the
    # caption stage has nothing to describe.
    options.generate_picture_images = True
    options.images_scale = IMAGE_SCALE
    return options


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def get_converter() -> DocumentConverter:
    """Built once per process: it loads several models."""
    logger.info("loading docling converter (layout, table structure, OCR)")
    converter = DocumentConverter(
        format_options={InputFormat.PDF: PdfFormatOption(pipeline_options=_pdf_options())}
    )
    logger.info("docling converter ready")
```

There's one setting that matters, and I measured it on the real table.

## The one that fixes qa-001

`HybridChunker(serializer_provider=...)` — swap the default `TripletTableSerializer` for `MarkdownTableSerializer`:

```python
class _MarkdownTables(ChunkingDocSerializer):
    table_serializer: BaseTableSerializer = MarkdownTableSerializer()

class _Provider(ChunkingSerializerProvider):
    def get_serializer(self, doc):
        return _MarkdownTables(doc=doc)

HybridChunker(tokenizer=tokenizer, serializer_provider=_Provider())
```

Re-chunking `aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx` with it produces:

```
| **Customer type** | **Return window** | **Condition**      | **Restocking fee** | **Refund method**       |
|-------------------|-------------------|--------------------|--------------------|-------------------------|
| Business          | 14 days           | Unopened           | None               | Credit note             |
| Business          | 14 days           | Opened, complete   | 15%                | Credit note             |
| Business          | 14 days           | Opened, incomplete | 30%                | Credit note             |
```

Rows are lines, and every cell sits under its own column. That's the addressability the work.md item asks for.

**It's also cheaper: 325 tokens vs 454 for the triplet form** — 28% fewer, on the same table. I expected a token cost and there isn't one; the triplet form repeats the row key and column name for every cell, markdown writes each once per row/table.

The pydantic detail: the override needs a type annotation (`table_serializer: BaseTableSerializer = ...`) or model construction raises.

## Knobs that interact with it

- **`omit_header_on_overflow`** (default `False`) — keep it. `MarkdownTableSerializer` implements `get_header_and_body_lines()` properly, so a table too big for 512 tokens splits row-wise with a real header + separator row repeated on each segment. Verified in `hybrid_chunker.segment()`; large-table behaviour improves too, not just this one.
- **`merge_peers`** (default `True`) — currently pulls the surrounding prose and the caption into the table's chunk, which is why the chunk reads well. Leave on; turning it off would isolate tables and strip that context.
- **`chunk_max_tokens`** = 512 — no change needed, the table lands at 325.

## Not worth touching

Parse side is already optimal: `do_table_structure=True` with `TableFormerMode.ACCURATE` and `do_cell_matching=True` (both are the defaults you're getting). And that layer only affects PDFs — the qa-001 table comes from DOCX, where structure comes from the file itself. The defect was never in table *extraction*, only in serialisation.

## Cost of doing it

Re-ingestion of the whole corpus (~194 s, mostly parse) since every table chunk's text and embedding change, plus a re-run of both golden sets. It touches `ingestion/chunk.py` only.

Want me to plan this out properly before implementing?

```
# Local development stack: api + qdrant as long-running services, ingestion as a
# one-shot job behind the `ingest` profile — see docs/work.md.
services:
  qdrant:
    # Keep in step with `qdrant-client` in pyproject.toml: the client warns and
    # may misbehave when the server's major/minor drift apart.
    image: qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.0
    restart: unless-stopped
    ports:
      # Exposed for inspection during development; the api reaches it over the
      # compose network regardless.
      - "6333:6333"
    volumes:
      - qdrant-storage:/qdrant/storage
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "bash -c ':> /dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/6333'"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 10s

  api:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.api
    restart: unless-stopped
    env_file: .env
    environment:
      # Service name on the compose network, not localhost.
      QDRANT_URL: http://qdrant:6333
      # The service appends its own subdirectory — see configure_logging.
      LOG_DIR: /app/logs
      # No HF_HOME and no model cache volume: BGE-M3 is baked into the image, and
      # a volume mounted over it would hide what the image carries.
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    volumes:
      # Development mount: source is live-editable and logs land on the host.
      # An upper environment would drop this and run the code baked into the
      # image — see docs/considerations.md.
      - ./api:/app/api
      - ./common:/app/common
      - ./logs:/app/logs
    command:
      - uvicorn
      - api.main:app
      - --host
      - "0.0.0.0"
      - --port
      - "8000"
      - --reload
    depends_on:
      qdrant:
        condition: service_healthy

  # A job, not a service: it runs the pipeline to completion and exits.
  #
  #   docker compose run --rm ingest              # ingest what changed
  #   docker compose run --rm ingest --dry-run    # show the plan, do nothing
  #
  # The `ingest` profile keeps it out of `docker compose up`, which would
  # otherwise start it on every stack boot.
  ingest:
    profiles: ["ingest"]
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.ingester
    env_file: .env
    environment:
      QDRANT_URL: http://qdrant:6333
      LOG_DIR: /app/logs
      CORPUS_DIR: /corpus
      # No HF_HOME and no model cache volume here: the ingester's models are
      # baked into its image, and a volume mounted over them would hide them.
      # ANTHROPIC_API_KEY arrives via env_file and is optional: it buys figure
      # descriptions. The cache for those lands under the ./logs mount, since
      # /corpus is read-only.
    volumes:
      # The initial set is the ingestion root; the later batch is copied in here
      # to demonstrate incremental ingestion. Read-only: the ingester parses
      # documents, it never writes them.
      - ./corpus/docs-initial:/corpus:ro
      - ./ingestion:/app/ingestion
      - ./common:/app/common
      - ./logs:/app/logs
    depends_on:
      qdrant:
        condition: service_healthy

volumes:
  qdrant-storage:
=== run.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run the app locally. Needs a .env with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (see .env.example).
# Set RELOAD=1 to restart on code changes.
# set -euo pipefail

# cd "$(dirname "$0")"

HOST="${HOST:-127.0.0.1}"
PORT="${PORT:-8000}"

args=(uv run uvicorn api.main:app --host "$HOST" --port "$PORT")
[ "${RELOAD:-0}" = "1" ] && args+=(--reload)

exec "${args[@]}"
```

Re-ingest complete. The corpus is now indexed with markdown tables.

**Run:** 20/20 documents, 553 s, 0 failures. **Zero Anthropic calls** — all 8 figures hit the description cache (`described=0 cached=1` on each).

**201 chunks, down from 208.** Markdown is more compact, so three table-heavy documents produce fewer chunks:

| document | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `fehlercode-referenz-de.pdf` | 58 | 53 |
| `accessory-catalogue-en.docx` | 6 | 5 |
| `aurora-installation-guide-en.pdf` | 23 | 22 |

That shrinkage needed manual cleanup. `ingest_document` writes points at `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)` and then calls `delete_stale(doc_id, content_hash)` — which removes points whose *hash* differs. The files didn't change, so the hash is identical and the 7 now-unused tail points weren't caught. I found them by comparing points written (201) against points present (208), confirmed 5 held stale triplet text and the other 2 were prose that survives at the preceding index (no content loss), then deleted them by id. Collection is at 201.

The returns table now sits in the index as a real markdown table — header, separator, and one line per row, with each `Business` row distinguishable by its `Condition` column.

**This is a latent bug, not a one-off.** Any change to chunking, serialisation, or the tokenizer shrinks some documents while their file hashes stay the same, and `--force` will silently leave orphans every time. The fix is for the forced path to delete by `doc_id` before re-indexing, or for `delete_stale` to also drop `chunk_index >= len(chunks)`. Want me to add that to `docs/work.md`, or fix it now?

Nothing committed yet, and no images were rebuilt (the ingester bind-mounts `./ingestion`), so there was nothing to prune — 0 dangling images, 938 G free.

Fixed. `delete_stale` now judges staleness on two measures instead of one.

**`ingestion/index.py`** — the filter is `doc_id` AND (hash differs OR `chunk_index >= chunk_count`), expressed as a `must` on `doc_id` with the two staleness conditions in `should`. New required `chunk_count` parameter, passed as `len(chunks)` from `runner.ingest_document`. Docstring explains why the hash alone is insufficient: a byte-identical file that re-chunks shorter is invisible to it, which is exactly what a serialiser or tokenizer change does to every document at once under `--force`.

**Verified on the real server**, not just embedded Qdrant — the `must` + `should` combination is the load-bearing semantic and I didn't want to trust the local implementation of it:

| filter | expected | got |
|---|---|---|
| current hash, count=53 | 0 (nothing stale) | 0 |
| current hash, count=50 | 3 (tail 50–52) | 3 |
| changed hash, count=53 | 53 (whole doc) | 53 |
| another document | untouched | 5 |

**Tests** — `tests/ingest/test_index.py`, 256 passing overall. Two new cases: a shorter re-chunk of an *unchanged* file leaves no orphans (the bug — fails without the fix), and a same-length version deletes nothing (the `>=` boundary, guarding against over-deletion). The three existing calls were passing `COLLECTION` positionally in the slot `chunk_count` now takes, so they're updated.

`docs/work.md` — moved to Done with the mechanism and the 7-point cost.

Note this fix would not have retroactively cleaned the orphans I removed by hand; it prevents the next occurrence. The index is correct at 201 points either way.

```
## Verification spikes — deferred

Several open questions about Docling's behaviour — whether `PictureItem.image`
populates for DOCX/HTML, whether `HybridChunker` emits pictures as their own
chunks, whether the PDF-only picture-description limits still hold in the pinned
version — are deliberately **not** investigated up front. We react if problems
surface.

The caveat worth being explicit about: two of these fail **silently**. If
pictures aren't emitted as chunks, nothing throws — images simply never appear
in retrieval results. So error monitoring won't catch them; the evaluation will,
via the golden questions whose answers exist only inside a chart image. That
makes the eval the real safety net for this deferral, which is fine as long as
it runs before ingestion is considered finished.

## AI usage logging

Did not consider making the ai logging necessary work for another dev or other
ai tools than Claude.

Known limitation: `scripts/export_chat_log.py` (+ `.githooks/pre-commit`) only
captures Claude Code sessions. It satisfies this PoC's own AI-usage transparency
requirement (requirements §10), but it is not a general multi-tool/multi-dev
trace capture — another dev, or a different AI assistant, would need separate
tooling.

## E2E testing (Playwright)

Not baked into a dev Docker image — the browser + its system libs (libnspr4,
libnss3, etc.) only matter for running e2e tests, not for running/reviewing
the app itself, so it'd be dead weight in the main image for this PoC. If
CI or multi-machine dev parity becomes a real need later, the right shape is
a separate test-only Dockerfile/service, not bundling it into the app image.

To install and run locally:

    uv sync --group dev                      # installs pytest, pytest-playwright
    uv run playwright install chromium       # downloads the Chromium browser binary
    sudo uv run playwright install-deps chromium   # installs required OS shared libs (needs interactive sudo)
    uv run pytest -m e2e -v

The `install-deps` step needs interactive sudo (apt-get under the hood), so
it can't be run non-interactively/by an agent — a human has to run it once
per machine. See `.claude/skills/e2e-testing/SKILL.md` for locator
conventions used in these tests.

## Improvements

Measured failures and their fixes; speculative ideas go in `docs/work.md`. One
line each, heading tagged `fixed` / `open`:

- **Broke** — the symptom, and what surfaced it
- **Why** — the cause
- **Fix** — what changed
- **Left** — residual risk or the next lever; omit when there is none

### Safety classifier invented a refusal category — fixed
```

```json
  "new_string": "## Known issues\n\nAccepted limits of the PoC, not defects. Each is a deliberate deferral with the\ncondition that would make it worth paying for.\n\n### 1. No real data\n\nThe corpus is synthetic \u2014 generated by `scripts/generate_corpus.py` to exercise\nformats, languages and layouts. Real documents bring inconsistent structure,\nscans, revisions and contradictions between sources. Every parsing and\nretrieval number here is measured against a corpus that was built to be\ntractable.\n\n### 2. Small, weak golden set\n\n51 questions written alongside the corpus, so they inherit its blind spots \u2014\nauthored by the same hand that knows where the answers live. Enough to catch\nregressions, not to establish accuracy. A real set is written from user\nquestions, by someone who did not build the index.\n\n### 3. No re-ranking\n\nFusion output goes straight to the model. On 201 chunks with distinct topics\nRRF is sufficient \u2014 measured MRR 0.931. It stops being sufficient at a corpus\nsize where many chunks are plausibly relevant and near-duplicates crowd the cut;\na cross-encoder over the top ~50 is the answer then, at the cost of a second\nmodel in the request path.\n\n### 4. Docker images are development-shaped\n\nSource is bind-mounted, `--reload` is on, and the images carry build tooling and\nbaked models. Convenient for a PoC and honest about it. Production needs the\ncode baked rather than mounted, non-root users, pinned digests, a slimmer\nruntime layer, and models on a shared volume or registry rather than inside\nevery image.\n\n### 5. No database behind the api\n\nThe api is stateless: history arrives from the client on each request and is\nnever persisted. That blocks server-side sessions (the client can forge history\n\u2014 hence guarding assistant turns), and it blocks the whole analytics loop.\nNothing accumulates about which questions get asked, which get refused, or which\nretrieve badly, so improvement stays anecdotal.\n\n### 6. No multi-turn evaluation\n\nEvery golden question is single-turn. A larger corpus invites questions that\nreference earlier answers, and evaluating those means driving multi-step\nconversations \u2014 agentic harness, tool development, and far higher eval cost per\nrun than the current single-shot pass.\n\n## Improvements\n\nMeasured failures and their fixes; speculative ideas go in `docs/work.md`."
}
```

```
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
#
# The api image. Deliberately excludes Docling and everything it drags in —
# parsing and chunking belong to the ingester, and no request path touches them
# (`uv sync --no-default-groups` installs main dependencies only).

FROM python:3.14-slim AS base

COPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /bin/uv

ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 \
    UV_COMPILE_BYTECODE=1 \
    UV_LINK_MODE=copy \
    # Outside /app so a bind-mounted working tree cannot shadow the venv.
    UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=/opt/venv

WORKDIR /app


FROM base AS deps

COPY pyproject.toml uv.lock ./
# --no-install-project: dependencies only, so this layer is cached until the
# lockfile changes rather than on every source edit.
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/uv \
    uv sync --frozen --no-default-groups --no-install-project


FROM base AS runtime

COPY --from=deps /opt/venv /opt/venv
ENV PATH="/opt/venv/bin:$PATH"

COPY api ./api
COPY common ./common

# BGE-M3 is baked in rather than fetched on first use: the api embeds the query
# before every search, so a first request that downloads 2.3 GB is not a warm-up
# cost, it is a timeout. Deliberately not under /models — that used to be a
# volume mount point, and a mounted volume shadows whatever the image carries.
ENV HF_HOME=/opt/models/huggingface

# No credentials here: `configure()` takes a settings *instance*, so the warmup
# builds a throwaway one in-process. Embedding reads only shared settings, and
# the api's real ApiSettings arrives from the environment at run time.
RUN python -c "\
from common.config import Settings, configure; \
configure(Settings(qdrant_url='http://unused-at-build-time')); \
from common.embedding import embed_documents; \
embed_documents(['warmup'])"

# The model directory stays root-owned — it is read-only at runtime.
RUN useradd --create-home --uid 1000 appuser \
    && mkdir -p /app/logs \
    && chown -R appuser:appuser /app
USER appuser

EXPOSE 8000

# Probes /health, not /: the chat page renders a template and mints a JWT on
# every request, which is a lot of work to answer "is the process up?".
HEALTHCHECK --interval=15s --timeout=5s --start-period=20s --retries=3 \
    CMD python -c "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8000/health').read()"

CMD ["uvicorn", "api.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]
```
</details>

Added `## Known issues` to `docs/considerations.md`, ahead of `## Improvements` — the two are distinct: Improvements logs measured failures that got fixed, this logs accepted limits. Each entry says what's missing and the condition that would make it worth paying for.

1. **No real data** — synthetic corpus built to be tractable; every parsing and retrieval number is measured against it
2. **Small, weak golden set** — 51 questions authored alongside the corpus, so they inherit its blind spots; enough for regressions, not for accuracy
3. **No re-ranking** — RRF suffices at 201 chunks (MRR 0.931); a cross-encoder over the top ~50 becomes the answer when near-duplicates crowd the cut
4. **Docker setup is development-shaped** — see below
5. **No database behind the api** — blocks server-side sessions (hence guarding client-supplied assistant turns) and blocks the analytics loop entirely, so improvement stays anecdotal
6. **No multi-turn evaluation** — needs an agentic harness, tool development, and much higher cost per eval run

One correction worth flagging: I first wrote item 4 as the images lacking non-root users and carrying build tooling. That's wrong — `Dockerfile.api` is multi-stage with `USER appuser`, a healthcheck, and code copied in. The actual gap is in how they're *run and sized*: `docker-compose.yml` bind-mounts `api/`, `common/`, `ingestion/` over the baked copies with `--reload`, so the image contents aren't what executes, and both images bake BGE-M3 (api ~16 GB) — correct locally, wrong for a registry. Also `ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest` is unpinned. Rewrote the entry to say that instead.

The MRR and question count are from the last recorded run and the corpus scripts, not from memory.

**Result**
```
| | Haiku 4.5 | Sonnet 5 / Opus 5 |
|---|---|---|
| Thinking | `{"type": "enabled", "budget_tokens": N}` / `"disabled"` | `{"type": "adaptive"}`; `budget_tokens` → 400 |
| `output_config.effort` | errors | `low` … `max` |
| `temperature` | allowed | → 400 |
| Context | 200K | 1M |
| Prompt-cache minimum | 4096 tokens | 512 (Opus 5) |

Consequences:

- The latency dial is **not** `effort` here (it doesn't exist on Haiku) — it is
  thinking configuration.
- `temperature` is available, a genuine lever for the "predictable response
  quality" requirement — but it disappears on upgrade, so nothing load-bearing
  should depend on it.
- **Prompt caching may silently do nothing.** Haiku's 4096-token minimum is high
  and a system prompt plus guardrails may fall below it. It doesn't error;
  `cache_creation_input_tokens` just stays 0. Assert `cache_read_input_tokens >
  0` rather than assuming.
- Keep model configuration in one place so the swap is a config change, not a
  hunt through the codebase.

### Citations — not built
store. A store would mean adding storage (an in-memory dict breaks the moment
more than one worker runs) for no PoC benefit, and statelessness keeps
horizontal scaling free. The costs are that the whole history must be validated
and guarded, and that turn count and total length need capping.

The LLM sees prior turns but **only the current turn's retrieved chunks** —
carrying previous turns' documents forward would grow context by roughly one
retrieval window per turn. Prompt order is system → history → retrieved
documents → question, keeping the volatile part last so the stable prefix stays
cacheable.

### Query rewriting — unconditional

A rewrite step runs before retrieval on every request, doing double duty:
resolving context-dependent follow-ups ("and what about returns?") and general
query improvement, which was wanted independently.
  protocol errors.

**The classifier is itself an injection target,** so the question and history are
sanitised through `api/rag/guardrails.py` before reaching it, and its system prompt
states that a message asking to be marked safe is itself unsafe.

**Not cached:** the classifier prompt is well under Haiku 4.5's 4096-token
prompt-cache minimum, so a `cache_control` breakpoint would silently no-op.

**Multi-turn is a nice-to-test, not a target.** History is supported, but
multi-turn behaviour isn't a graded goal — the golden Q&A set is single-turn.
The consequence to be aware of is that the follow-up path ships largely
untested.
```
</details>

## The item

```
- Assert `cache_read_input_tokens > 0` in a test — still blocked: Haiku 4.5's
  prompt-cache minimum is 4096 tokens and a real request now measures ~2 900
  input tokens for generation, so the prefix is still short of it. Neighbour
  expansion may push it over
```

It comes from `docs/considerations.md:443` — "prompt caching may silently do nothing… assert `cache_read_input_tokens > 0` rather than assuming." The intent was a guard against a silent no-op: caching doesn't error when it fails, `cache_creation_input_tokens` just stays 0.

## Two things make it unreachable, and the note names neither

**1. Caching is never requested.** There is no `cache_control` breakpoint anywhere in `api/rag/llm.py` — the call is `model`, `max_tokens`, `system`, `messages`. `api/core/timings.py` *records* `cache_read_input_tokens` and the performance log prints `cache_read=`, but nothing asks the API to cache. So the assertion tests a feature that was never turned on, and would fail at any token count.

**2. The blocker is the shared prefix, not total input.** Caching matches a prefix across requests. Here the prompt is system → history → retrieved documents → question. Only the system prompt is stable between two different questions — 1058 chars, roughly 250–300 tokens. History, documents and question are unique per request, so they cannot participate no matter how large they get.

That makes the note's reasoning wrong in a way that matters:

- "~2 900 input tokens… still short of 4096" measures the wrong quantity. Total input at k=5 runs 1 348–2 450 in recent logs, but the *cacheable* portion is ~264 either way — an order of magnitude under the floor, not just short of it.
- "Neighbour expansion may push it over" won't. Expansion grows the retrieved documents, which are per-request. Same for the `retrieval_top_k` 5 → 10 I just made: total generation input roughly doubles, the shared prefix stays ~264.

The analysis call is the better cache candidate and still fails — its input is a stable 859–878 tokens (classifier prompt ~456), which `considerations.md:624` already records as under the floor.

## Options

| | Option | Cost | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | **Close as won't-do**, correct the two premises in the docs | ~10 min | Recommended |
| B | Pad the system prompt past 4 096 tokens to make it cacheable | Pay ~3 800 unnecessary input tokens *every* request, plus 1.25× on cache writes, to save 0.9× on hits | Inverted economics at PoC volume |
| C | Move generation to Sonnet 5 / Opus 5 (512-token minimum) | Still above the ~264 prefix, so it needs B anyway; changes the model for cache economics | No |
| D | Enable `cache_control` and assert it's *inert* — that `cache_read` stays 0 and nothing errors | Small | Documents a no-op, tests nothing useful |
| E | Keep the plumbing test only — `TokenUsage.from_sdk` already has one at `tests/unit/test_timings.py:87` | Zero, already exists | Pairs with A |

**A + E.** The guard was worth writing down when caching looked like a config detail away; it isn't. The honest closure is that prompt caching cannot pay off in this architecture — a per-request RAG prompt where the only stable prefix is a 264-token system message — and that's true of the design, not of Haiku's floor. It becomes live only if a large static block (extensive few-shot examples, a fixed policy preamble) ever legitimately enters the prefix.

Want me to close it that way and fix the `considerations.md:443` and `:624` wording?

```
from common.logging_config import configure_logging


@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
    # First, and before anything reads configuration: `common/` has no settings
    # of its own and serves whichever child the entrypoint injects. Building
    # ApiSettings here also fails fast on missing or invalid configuration.
    configure(ApiSettings())
    configure_logging("api")
    vector_store.check_connection()  # fail fast if the vector store is unreachable
    # Load BGE-M3 now rather than on the first search. The weights are baked into
    # the image, but loading them still takes seconds — paid once at startup here
    # instead of by whoever asks the first question. Off the event loop, since it
    # is CPU-bound.
    await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(get_embedder)
    yield


app = FastAPI(title="chat-bot", lifespan=lifespan)
app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=STATIC_DIR), name="static")
app.include_router(pages.router)
=== measure_retrieval ===
async def measure_retrieval(items: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    """Recall and rank for each question, over the production query path."""
    from api.rag.query_analysis import analyse_query
    from api.rag.retriever import RetrievalQueries, retrieve

    rows = []
    for index, item in enumerate(items, 1):
        analysis = await analyse_query(item["question"])
        started = time.perf_counter()
        chunks = await retrieve(
            RetrievalQueries(
                original=item["question"],
                rewritten=analysis.rewritten_query,
                keywords=tuple(analysis.keywords),
                sub_queries=tuple(analysis.sub_queries),
            )
        )
        elapsed = time.perf_counter() - started

        # doc_id is the path relative to the corpus root; the golden set names
        # the file only.
        retrieved = [Path(c.doc_id).name for c in chunks]
        wanted, mode = wanted_documents(item["source"])
        ranks = {d: retrieved.index(d) + 1 for d in wanted if d in retrieved}
        if mode == "all":
            # Answerable only once every required document is in hand, so the
            # rank that matters is the deepest one.
            rank = max(ranks.values()) if len(ranks) == len(wanted) else None
        else:
            rank = min(ranks.values()) if ranks else None
        rows.append(
            {
                "id": item["id"],
                "type": item["type"],
                "wanted": sorted(wanted),
                "mode": mode,
                "rank": rank,
                "found": sorted(ranks),
                "retrieved": retrieved,
                "seconds": round(elapsed, 3),
            }
        )
        print(
            f"{index:2}/{len(items)} {item['id']} "
            f"{'rank ' + str(rank) if rank else 'MISS '} ({item['type']}, {elapsed * 1000:.0f}ms)",
```

Checked both. You're right on one, half right on the other.

## Warming — still open, and the api warmup isn't the same thing

The api *does* warm: `api/main.py:29` calls `get_embedder` in the lifespan, off the event loop. That's the Backend item already marked done.

But the Testing item is about the **harness's own retrieval pass**, which is a separate process. `scripts/eval_golden.py:426 measure_retrieval` imports `api.rag.retriever` and calls `retrieve()` directly — it never touches the api's lifespan, so BGE-M3 loads lazily inside the first call. The last run shows it plainly:

| question | retrieval |
|---|---|
| qa-001 | **5.854 s** |
| qa-002 | 0.071 s |
| qa-003 | 0.045 s |
| median | 0.183 s |

That 5.854 s is model load, not retrieval. So the item stands as written — including its own caveat that the median is unaffected and only the per-question table misleads. One `get_embedder()` before the loop fixes it.

## Single-prompt latency — measured, never asserted

Also as written. The harness prints `latency median 2.9s, slowest 5.3s` (`eval_golden.py:569`) and writes per-stage medians/p95/max to the report, but the exit code is `return 0 if not failures else 1` where `failures` is answer-correctness only. No 5 s threshold exists anywhere in the file.

Worth knowing before adding one: the last run's slowest answer was **5.3 s**, over the §7.2 budget. A naive `assert slowest < 5` fails today on one question out of 26. An assertion on the median (2.9 s) or on p95 would pass — which of those you want is a real choice, not a detail.

So neither item is done. What's actually true is narrower than the items suggest, and I'd reword them:

- *Warm the embedder in the harness's retrieval pass* — say "harness" explicitly, since the api half is done and the current wording reads like it isn't
- *Assert a latency budget in the harness* — say that measurement exists and only the assertion is missing, and note the 5.3 s outlier so whoever picks the threshold isn't surprised

Want me to apply those rewordings, or fix the warmup now — it's a two-line change?

Corpus-size\ndependent, like re-ranking: it matters when an answer needs more context than\none chunk holds *and* the adjoining chunk did not make the cut. Raising\n`retrieval_top_k` to 10 covers the cheap end of that. `parent_id` is in the\npayload and neighbour ids are computable from `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)`, so\nthe work is ready to start \u2014 `point_id()` has to leave `ingestion/index.py`\nfirst, since that module imports docling and must not reach the api.\n\n### 8. No rate limiting\n\n`/api/chat` is public and unmetered. Every message costs an analysis call\n*before* anything can refuse it, so an attacker generates real spend with no\naccumulating penalty \u2014 a refused message is kept out of the history, so retrying\nfrom a clean conversation costs them nothing. The page token is a demonstration,\nnot authentication, and does not bound this.\n\nThe fix is not a limiter bolted onto an open endpoint: it is real user\nauthentication, the api no longer publicly reachable, and a per-user limit on\ntop. Accepted for a PoC that is not deployed.\n\n### 9. No linting, type checking, CI or coverage\n\nRuff, mypy, pre-commit, CI and coverage reporting are all absent \u2014 they buy\nconsistency and safe collaboration rather than working software, which is why\nthey lost to features on a timed solo PoC and would not on a team.\n\n## Improvements"

Trigger is measured failure, not preference. When it lands: neighbour ids are computable from `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)`, so it is a keyed `retrieve()` rather than a filtered scan; merge overlapping windows and clamp to `parent_id`. `point_id()` has to move out of `ingestion/index.py` first \u2014 that module imports docling, which must not reach the api\n- Rate limiting on `/api/chat` \u2014 a refused message is kept out of the history, so an attacker retries from a clean conversation with no accumulating penalty\n\n**Tooling not set up yet** \u2014 none of it changes behaviour, which is why it lost to features on a timed PoC:\n- **Linting and formatting** (ruff). Known issues is now nine, and the Deferred section is fully closed.

**#7 No neighbour expansion** — corpus-size dependent like re-ranking: it matters only when an answer needs more than one chunk holds *and* the adjoining chunk missed the cut. Noted that `retrieval_top_k` 10 covers the cheap end, and kept the two implementation facts worth not rediscovering: `parent_id` is already in the payload, and `point_id()` must leave `ingestion/index.py` first because that module imports docling.

**#8 No rate limiting** — framed as the security issue you described. The sharp edge is that the analysis call happens *before* anything can refuse, so an attacker spends real money on messages that get refused, and the refusal is kept out of history so retrying from a clean conversation carries no penalty. The page token is explicitly not a bound on this. Fix stated as auth + not publicly reachable + per-user limit, not a limiter on an open endpoint.

**#9 No linting, type checking, CI or coverage** — one line: consistency and safe collaboration rather than working software, which is why it lost on a timed solo PoC and wouldn't on a team.

`work.md` Deferred now holds three struck lines pointing at #7/#8/#9, with the six tooling bullets collapsed into one (the `# noqa: E402` detail kept, since it's a real fact about the code).

**Result**
```
### Vector DB — Qdrant

Candidates narrowed to Qdrant and Weaviate: both ship an official Docker image
and both advertise hybrid search. Chroma was dropped once BGE-M3 was chosen,
because the sparse-vector requirement rules it out.

**The decisive difference is what "hybrid" means in each product.**

- **Weaviate** hybrid = dense vector (bring your own) fused with **Weaviate's own
  internal BM25F** inverted index over object properties. The `hybrid()` query
  takes `query`, `vector`, `alpha`, `bm25_operator`, `query_properties` — there
  is no parameter for a precomputed sparse vector. You cannot feed it BGE-M3's
  sparse output.
- **Qdrant** treats sparse vectors as a first-class type, and the Query API
  fuses dense + sparse server-side in a single request via `prefetch` with RRF
  or DBSF.

Since BGE-M3 was chosen precisely *because* it emits dense and sparse from one
model, Weaviate would mean throwing half of it away — and with it the
justification for picking BGE-M3 at all.

Beyond that:

### Embedding model — BGE-M3

Options considered: `multilingual-e5-small`, `multilingual-e5-large`, BGE-M3,
Cohere multilingual embeddings.

- **Cohere — excluded.** Free-tier rate limits make a reviewer's
  `docker compose up` unreproducible, and it adds a second API vendor plus
  another key to manage.
- **e5-small — considered, then rejected.** The initial instinct was the usual
  upgrade-on-evidence policy: start with the smallest model, which also keeps
  image size down. Rejected because it only covers the *dense* half of
  retrieval — pairing it with a separate BM25/lexical component to get hybrid
  search means more moving parts to build and maintain.
- **BGE-M3 — chosen.** Emits dense **and** sparse vectors from a single model,
  so hybrid retrieval works out of the box and there is no second lexical path
  to hand-roll. Multilingual and cross-lingual by design, which is what the ~20%
  EN/DE content overlap in the corpus is meant to exercise. 8k token input
  limit, so chunk size is not constrained by the embedder (e5's 512-token
  ceiling would have capped it).
### LLM — Claude Haiku 4.5 to start

Upgrade-on-evidence again: cheapest and fastest first. It is also the best fit
for the ≤5s target, and for grounded extractive answering over retrieved context
the hard problem is retrieval, not generation — which is where the complexity
budget went.

Upgrade path is Sonnet 5, then Opus 5. Expect the ceiling to show up in
multi-hop synthesis across several chunks and in nuanced German phrasing, rather
than in simple extraction — so the golden Q&A set needs both, or the ceiling
stays invisible.

**Haiku 4.5 is an older-generation model and its request surface differs from
```
</details>

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/docs/architecture.md",
  "content": "# Architecture\n\nReasoning lives in `docs/considerations.md`; this is the shape of the system.\n\n## Deployment units\n\nTwo long-running services and a job, via `docker-compose`. No load balancer.\n\n```\n        browser\n           \u2502  HTTP + streamed text\n           \u25bc\n    \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510        \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n    \u2502     api     \u2502\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u25b6\u2502  qdrant  \u2502\u25c0\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n    \u2502  FastAPI    \u2502 search \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518 upsert \u2502\n    \u2502  + BGE-M3   \u2502                            \u2502\n    \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518                     \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n           \u2502 HTTPS                      \u2502   ingest    \u2502\n           \u25bc                            \u2502  job, batch \u2502\n     Anthropic API                      \u2502  + Docling  \u2502\n           \u25b2                            \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n           \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500 figure descriptions \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518 \n```\n\n| Unit | Kind | Owns |\n|---|---|---|\n| **api** | service | frontend, query analysis, embedding, search, generation |\n| **qdrant** | service | vectors + chunk payloads, on a named volume |\n| **ingest** | one-shot job | Docling parsing, figure description, chunking, index-time embedding |\n\nIngestion is minutes of pinned CPU, so it never shares a container with the\nrequest path. It has **no network surface**: no endpoint, no path argument\n(the root is the `CORPUS_DIR` setting), corpus mounted read-only.\n\nBGE-M3 lives in both images \u2014 index-time and query-time embeddings must come\nfrom the same model.\n\n## Code layout\n\n| Package | Deployed in | Contents |\n|---|---|---|\n| `api/` | api image | `routers/` (paths only), `services/` (flow), `rag/` (analysis, retrieval, generation, guardrails), `core/` (config, schemas, constants, security, timings) |\n| `ingestion/` | ingest image | discovery, state, parse, describe, chunk, index, runner |\n| `common/` | both | config base, embedding, vector store, logging, request context |\n\n`common/` owns no settings instance. Each entrypoint injects its own child \u2014\n`ApiSettings` or `IngestSettings` \u2014 through `configure()`, so the ingest job\nneeds no Anthropic key or JWT secret.\n\n## Query pipeline\n\n```\nuser message + history\n  \u2192 analysis        one Haiku call: safety verdict + query rewrite (keywords, sub-queries)\n  \u2192 embed           BGE-M3, one batched pass over every branch, off the event loop\n  \u2192 search          Qdrant Query API: dense + sparse prefetch per branch (20), RRF fused, top 10\n  \u2192 generate        Claude Haiku 4.5, streamed to the client\n```\n\nAnalysis is awaited before the response starts, because the outcome must be\nsettled before headers are sent. Retrieval and generation then run inside the\nstream.\n\n- **Fails closed** \u2014 no usable safety verdict means refuse, without retrieving.\n- **Degrades, not 500s** \u2014 unreachable Qdrant returns no chunks and the model\n  says it cannot answer.\n- Prompt order is system \u2192 history \u2192 documents \u2192 question.\n- The model sees prior turns but only the *current* turn's chunks.\n\nOutcomes (`answered` / `refused` / `unavailable`) return as `X-Chat-Outcome`,\npaired with `X-Request-ID` that ties a reply to its log lines.\n\n## Ingestion pipeline\n\n```\ndiscover \u2192 plan \u2192 parse \u2192 describe \u2192 chunk \u2192 embed \u2192 index \u2192 delete stale\n```\n\nOne document end to end at a time; a failure costs that document, logged at\nERROR with the doc_id and the stage.\n\n- **Plan** is read from the collection itself, not a side file, so the record\n  cannot disagree with the index. New / changed / unchanged / deleted.\n- **Describe** runs before chunking \u2014 a figure's description is folded into the\n  chunk text. Cached by image hash, so re-runs cost nothing.\n- **Index** writes all of a document's points in one `wait=True` upsert at\n  `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)`, then deletes what is stale by content hash or\n  chunk count.\n\n20 documents \u2192 **201 chunks**. Parsing dominates the runtime.\n\n## State\n\nNothing is stored server-side except vectors. Conversation history arrives from\nthe client on every request, capped at 10 turns / 10 000 chars / 4 000 per turn,\nand every turn is validated and guarded \u2014 assistant turns included, since a\ncaller can forge them.\n\n## Chunk payload\n\n`doc_id`, `chunk_index`, `parent_id`, `page_numbers`, `headings`,\n`source_format`, `doc_content_hash`, and the chunk `text` \u2014 so retrieval answers\nin one round trip and the api never mounts the corpus.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/docs/design-decisions.md",
  "content": "# Key design decisions and trade-offs\n\nOne line of reasoning each; the long form is in `docs/considerations.md`.\n\n## Retrieval\n\n| Decision | Why | Trade-off |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Hybrid dense + sparse, RRF fused** | An error code like `F250` has to survive as a term, not be smeared into a topic vector | Two vectors per chunk |\n| **One model for both vectors** (BGE-M3) | Hybrid costs one model and one forward pass, with no second lexical path to hand-roll | 2.3 GB in both images |\n| **Prefetch 20, top-k 10** | RRF can only rank what the branches surfaced, so the shortlist is wider than the cut | Context tokens grow with k |\n| **Original query always a branch** | A rewrite that drifts cannot retrieve worse than the raw question | One extra branch per request |\n| **No re-ranking** | RRF suffices over 201 topically distinct chunks (MRR 0.931) | Nothing checks a chunk *answers* rather than resembles |\n| **No neighbour expansion** | No answer yet fails *because* a chunk was a fragment | Fragments stay possible; k=10 is the cheap mitigation |\n\n## Ingestion\n\n| Decision | Why | Trade-off |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Docling for all three formats** | One parser, one document tree, one chunker \u2014 PDF/DOCX/HTML differences stay inside it | Heavy: torch, ~8 min image builds |\n| **A job, not a service** | It runs to completion in minutes and needs no network surface at all | Started by an operator or a schedule |\n| **State read from the collection** | The record cannot disagree with the index | Requires a live Qdrant to plan a run |\n| **Deterministic ids + one upsert** | A document is present at a hash or absent, never half-written | Shrinking documents need explicit stale deletion |\n| **Describe every figure from pixels** | Chart values exist only in the image; `qa-005`/`qa-007` cannot be answered otherwise | A vision call per new figure, cached by image hash |\n| **Markdown tables, not Docling triplets** | Triplets key every cell on the first column, so rows differing late read alike | None \u2014 markdown is also 28% cheaper in tokens |\n| **OCR on** | Reads text rendered inside images, which the captioner transcribes unreliably | Slower parsing |\n\n## Generation\n\n| Decision | Why | Trade-off |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Claude Haiku 4.5** | Cheapest and fastest first; for grounded extraction the hard problem is retrieval | Ceiling shows in multi-hop synthesis and nuanced German |\n| **Safety verdict + rewrite in one call** | Both jobs, one round trip, before retrieval | A single call to fail closed on |\n| **Fails closed** | No usable verdict means refuse \u2014 never generate ungoverned | A model outage becomes a refusal |\n| **Streamed** | TTFT is what the user feels, and the rewrite already spends time before it | Outcome must be settled before headers go out |\n| **No citations** | Out of scope | No provenance in answers; logs keep it |\n| **No prompt caching** | The only shared prefix is a ~264-token system prompt, under Haiku's 4096 floor | None |\n\n## Backend\n\n| Decision | Why | Trade-off |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Stateless history** | No store to add, and horizontal scaling stays free | The whole history is user input and must be validated and guarded |\n| **Guardrails on user input only** | Indexed documents are trusted for a PoC | A poisoned corpus is undefended |\n| **Caps: 10 turns / 10k chars** | Bounds cost and context per request; the client trims before sending | Long conversations lose their oldest turns |\n| **Settings injected, not imported** | `common/` serves whichever child the entrypoint provides, so the ingest job carries no api credentials | One `configure()` call per entrypoint |\n| **JWT in the page** | Demonstrates the mechanism end to end | **Not authentication** \u2014 anyone loading the page gets one |\n| **Two log streams** | Ordinary logs at INFO, timings at DEBUG, separate files | Two files to read |\n\n## Verification\n\n| Decision | Why | Trade-off |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Golden Q&A set, LLM-judged** | Turns \"high accuracy\" into a number that moves when the system changes | The set is synthetic and shares the corpus's blind spots |\n| **Judge on Opus 5, not Haiku** | Haiku graded three correct answers wrong, moving the score 21/21 \u2192 18/21 with no change to the system | ~50 judge calls per run |\n| **Retrieval scored on the production path** | `analyse_query()` then every branch it produces, so the figure covers what is actually served | Needs Qdrant and BGE-M3 locally |\n| **Embedded Qdrant in unit tests** | Real retrieval exercised without Docker | Payload indexes are ignored there and verified against the real server |\n| **Latency measured, not asserted** | Median, slowest and per-stage p95 are reported every run | No build fails on a slow answer |\n\n## Last measured (k=5, before the markdown-table change)\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| Answer correctness | 21/21 on the ingested corpus |\n| Retrieval recall@5 | 18/18, MRR 0.931 |\n| Answer latency | median 2.9 s, slowest 5.3 s |\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "# Models, tools and technologies\n\nVersions are what the lockfile currently resolves. Why each was chosen is in\n`docs/design-decisions.md`.\n\n## Models\n\n| Role | Model | Where it runs |\n|---|---|---|\n| Embeddings | **BGE-M3** (`BAAI/bge-m3`) \u2014 dense + sparse from one forward pass, multilingual, 8k input | Local, baked into both images |\n| Generation | **Claude Haiku 4.5** (`claude-haiku-4-5`) | Anthropic API |\n| Query analysis | **Claude Haiku 4.5**, structured output | Anthropic API |\n| Figure description | **Claude Haiku 4.5** vision, via the OpenAI-compatible endpoint Docling's API describer speaks | Anthropic API |\n| Eval judge | **Claude Opus 5** (`claude-opus-5`) | Anthropic API, eval only |\n\nAlternatives weighed: `multilingual-e5-small/large` and Cohere embeddings;\nWeaviate and Chroma for the store; Sonnet 5 / Opus 5 as the generation upgrade\npath.\n\n## Runtime\n\n| Layer | Choice | Version |\n|---|---|---|\n| Language | Python | 3.14 |\n| Web framework | FastAPI + uvicorn (async) | 0.141.1 / 0.52.1 |\n| Frontend | Jinja2 templates, plain HTML + vanilla JS, no build step | 3.1.6 |\n| Vector DB | Qdrant (server + `qdrant-client`, kept in step) | 1.19.0 |\n| Embeddings runtime | `FlagEmbedding` on torch | 1.4.0 / 2.13.0 |\n| LLM SDK | `anthropic` | 0.121.0 |\n| Parsing | Docling / `docling-core` | 2.119.0 / 2.91.0 |\n| Config | pydantic + pydantic-settings | 2.13.4 / 2.15.0 |\n| Auth token | PyJWT (HS256) | 2.13.0 |\n| Async | anyio | 4.14.2 |\n\n## Tooling\n\n| Purpose | Tool |\n|---|---|\n| Dependencies + venv | `uv`, `uv.lock`, dependency groups (`ingest`, `dev`) |\n| Packaging | Docker, multi-stage, non-root; `docker-compose` for the stack |\n| Tests | pytest, `pytest-playwright` (e2e), httpx |\n| Eval | `scripts/eval_golden.py` \u2014 answer correctness + retrieval metrics |\n| Corpus | `scripts/generate_corpus.py` \u2014 byte-identical regeneration |\n\nMarkers keep the slow paths out of the default run: `docling` (real parsing),\n`ingest`, `e2e`.\n\nNot set up: ruff, mypy, pre-commit, CI, coverage \u2014 see `docs/considerations.md`\n\u2192 Known issues #9.\n\n## Commands\n\n```bash\ndocker compose up                      # api + qdrant\ndocker compose run --rm ingest         # ingest what changed\ndocker compose run --rm ingest --force # re-process everything\nuv run pytest -q                       # fast suite\nuv run pytest -m docling               # real documents\npython -m scripts.eval_golden          # golden Q&A, needs the stack up\n```\n"
}
```

Yes — you already have it, and it's live right now.

| endpoint | status |
|---|---|
| `/docs` | 200 — Swagger UI, interactive |
| `/redoc` | 200 — ReDoc, reference style |
| `/openapi.json` | 200 — OpenAPI 3.1 schema |

FastAPI generates all three from the route signatures and Pydantic models, so `ChatRequest`'s shape, its caps, and the 422 are already documented with no work. That covers the "try swagger or another way of docing the API" item essentially for free.

Three gaps if you want it to be actually usable rather than just present:

**1. The token is invisible to Swagger.** `verify_token` takes a raw `Request` and reads the header itself, so nothing appears in `components.securitySchemes` and `/api/chat` has no `security` entry. "Try it out" will 401 with no field to paste a token into. Fix is `HTTPBearer` as the dependency — same check, but it registers in the schema and Swagger grows an **Authorize** button. Small change in `api/core/security.py`.

**2. `/api/chat` documents almost nothing.** Only 200 and 422 are listed; the 401 isn't, the response is described as JSON rather than a `text/plain` stream, and `X-Chat-Outcome` / `X-Request-ID` don't appear at all — those are the part a client integrator actually needs. All fixable with `responses={...}` and a docstring on the route, no logic change.

**3. Swagger UI loads from a CDN.** The page pulls `swagger-ui-bundle.js` and `swagger-ui.css` from `cdn.jsdelivr.net`. Fine on a connected machine; for a zip deliverable opened offline, `/docs` renders blank. ReDoc has the same issue. Self-hosting the assets is a known FastAPI recipe but means vendoring ~1 MB into the image.

My suggestion: do 1 and 2 (maybe an hour, and they're the difference between a schema and usable docs), skip 3 unless offline review matters — and note in `docs/technologies.md` that `/docs` exists, since right now nothing tells a reader it's there.

Want me to do that?

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"Routing only: paths and methods onto the code that handles them, plus the\ndependencies each route needs. Anything a request *does* belongs in a service.\n\"\"\"\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Response\nfrom fastapi.responses import PlainTextResponse\n\nfrom api.core.constants import OUTCOME_HEADER, REQUEST_ID_HEADER\nfrom api.core.schemas import ChatRequest\nfrom api.core.security import verify_token\nfrom api.services.chat import respond\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/api\")\n\n# The reply is a stream of plain text, and the two headers are the only way a\n# client learns what happened \u2014 neither is inferable from the return type, so\n# both are spelled out here. 422 is left to FastAPI, which documents it from\n# `ChatRequest`.\n_CHAT_RESPONSES = {\n    200: {\n        \"description\": (\n            \"The reply, streamed as plain text. A refusal and an unavailable \"\n            \"service also arrive as 200 \u2014 they are content decisions, not \"\n            \"protocol errors. Read the outcome header to tell them apart.\"\n        ),\n        \"content\": {\"text/plain\": {\"schema\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}},\n        \"headers\": {\n            OUTCOME_HEADER: {\n                \"description\": (\n                    \"`answered`, `refused` or `unavailable`. Only an `answered` \"\n                    \"exchange belongs in the history sent back on the next \"\n                    \"request. Sent before the stream is iterated, so a \"\n                    \"generation that fails part-way still reads `answered`.\"\n                ),\n                \"schema\": {\n                    \"type\": \"string\",\n                    \"enum\": [\"answered\", \"refused\", \"unavailable\"],\n                },\n            },\n            REQUEST_ID_HEADER: {\n                \"description\": \"Ties this reply to its server log lines.\",\n                \"schema\": {\"type\": \"string\"},\n            },\n        },\n    },\n    401: {\"description\": \"Missing, malformed or expired page token.\"},\n}\n\n\n@router.post(\n    \"/chat\",\n    dependencies=[Depends(verify_token)],\n    summary=\"Ask a question about the corpus\",\n    response_class=PlainTextResponse,\n    responses=_CHAT_RESPONSES,\n)\nasync def chat(request: ChatRequest) -> Response:\n    \"\"\"Answer one message, grounded in the indexed documents.\n\n    The message is classified for safety and rewritten in a single call, then\n    retrieval runs on every branch the rewrite produced and the answer streams\n    back as it is generated.\n\n    History is supplied by the client on every request \u2014 there is no server-side\n    session. It is capped at 10 turns, 10 000 characters in total and 4 000 per\n    turn, and must begin with a `user` turn; anything else is a 422.\n    \"\"\"\n    return await respond(request)\n"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta\n\nimport jwt\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException, Request\nfrom fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials\n\nfrom api.core.constants import JWT_ALGORITHM\nfrom api.core.security import bearer_scheme, issue_token, verify_token\nfrom api.core.config import get_settings\n\n\ndef _request(headers: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> Request:\n    raw = [(key.lower().encode(), value.encode()) for key, value in (headers or {}).items()]\n    return Request({\"type\": \"http\", \"method\": \"POST\", \"path\": \"/api/chat\", \"headers\": raw})\n\n\ndef _bearer(token: str) -> HTTPAuthorizationCredentials:\n    \"\"\"What `bearer_scheme` hands the dependency once the header has parsed.\"\"\"\n    return HTTPAuthorizationCredentials(scheme=\"Bearer\", credentials=token)\n\n\nasync def _check_header(headers: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> None:\n    \"\"\"The real chain: the security scheme parses, then the dependency verifies.\n\n    Header shape is `HTTPBearer`'s job since it became a declared scheme, so\n    these go through it rather than calling `verify_token` with a raw request.\n    \"\"\"\n    verify_token(await bearer_scheme(_request(headers)))"
```

```json
  "new_string": "@pytest.mark.anyio\nasync def test_missing_or_malformed_header_is_rejected(headers: dict[str, str]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Still a 401, not `HTTPBearer`'s own 403 \u2014 `auto_error` is off so this\n    module keeps its answer and its `WWW-Authenticate` header.\"\"\"\n    with pytest.raises(HTTPException) as error:\n        await _check_header(headers)\n\n    assert error.value.status_code == 401\n\n\n@pytest.mark.anyio\nasync def test_bearer_scheme_is_case_insensitive() -> None:\n    await _check_header({\"Authorization\": f\"bearer {issue_token()}\"})\n\n\n@pytest.mark.anyio\nasync def test_rejection_does_not_leak_the_reason_to_the_client() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(HTTPException) as error:\n        await _check_header()\n\n    assert error.value.detail == \"invalid or expired token\""
```

Both done, verified against the live schema.

**1. Token declared as a security scheme** — `api/core/security.py` now uses `HTTPBearer(auto_error=False, scheme_name="PageToken")` as a dependency instead of reading the raw `Request`. `auto_error=False` matters: the scheme's own rejection is a bare 403, and turning it off keeps this module's 401 with `WWW-Authenticate` and the logged reason. Confirmed on the wire — no token still returns 401 with `www-authenticate: Bearer`.

`/docs` now has an **Authorize** button, with the description explaining where to get a token and that it isn't authentication.

**2. `/api/chat` documents what a client needs** — summary, a docstring description covering the flow and the history caps, and:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| responses | 200, 422 | 200, **401**, 422 |
| 200 content | `application/json` | `text/plain` |
| headers | none | `X-Chat-Outcome` (enum: answered/refused/unavailable), `X-Request-ID` |

The 200 description states the thing that isn't guessable — a refusal and an unavailable service also arrive as 200, so the header is how you tell them apart. 422 is left to FastAPI, which documents it from `ChatRequest`.

**Tests** — 256 pass. Twelve failed initially because `verify_token`'s signature changed and several tests fed it a hand-built `Request`. Rather than patch them to keep working, I split them by what they actually test now: token validity calls `verify_token` with `HTTPAuthorizationCredentials`, and header-shape cases (missing, malformed, wrong scheme, case-insensitivity) go through `bearer_scheme` first, since parsing is `HTTPBearer`'s job now. Same coverage, aimed at the real chain.

Also added `REQUEST_ID_HEADER` to `constants.py` — `X-Request-ID` was a bare literal in `chat.py`, and the schema would have been a second copy.

`docs/technologies.md` and the work.md item both note the CDN dependency, since you skipped self-hosting: `/docs` renders blank offline.

The run crashed at question 42 of 51 — **the page token expired mid-run**. `JWT_TTL_SECONDS` is 1800 and the harness mints one token at startup; retrieval plus 41 answers took longer than 30 minutes, so `/api/chat` started returning 401.

<details><summary>tool_use: Bash — cd /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot; ls -la --time-style=+%H:%M logs/common...</summary>

```json
}
```

```
def must_decline(item: dict) -> bool:
    """`later` documents are not in the ingestion root, so answering is invention."""
    return item["type"] == "unanswerable" or item["batch"] == "later"


def get_token(client: httpx.Client, base: str) -> str:
    match = TOKEN_META.search(client.get(f"{base}/").text)
    if not match:
        sys.exit(f"no chat token at {base}/ — is the stack up?")
    return match.group(1)


def ask(client: httpx.Client, base: str, token: str, question: str) -> tuple[str, str, str, float]:
    started = time.perf_counter()
    response = client.post(
        f"{base}/api/chat",
        json={"message": question},
        headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"},
        timeout=180.0,
    )
    response.raise_for_status()
    return (
        response.text.strip(),
        response.headers.get("X-Chat-Outcome", "?"),
        # Joins this answer to its per-stage timings in the performance log.
        response.headers.get("X-Request-ID", ""),
        time.perf_counter() - started,
    )
```
</details>

```json
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "def get_token(client: httpx.Client, base: str) -> str:\n    match = TOKEN_META.search(client.get(f\"{base}/\").text)\n    if not match:\n        sys.exit(f\"no chat token at {base}/ \u2014 is the stack up?\")\n    return match.group(1)",
  "new_string": "def get_token(client: httpx.Client, base: str) -> str:\n    match = TOKEN_META.search(client.get(f\"{base}/\").text)\n    if not match:\n        sys.exit(f\"no chat token at {base}/ \u2014 is the stack up?\")\n    return match.group(1)\n\n\nclass PageToken:\n    \"\"\"The page token, re-mintable.\n\n    Held rather than passed as a string because it expires: `JWT_TTL_SECONDS`\n    defaults to 1800 and a full run over both sets takes longer than that.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, client: httpx.Client, base: str) -> None:\n        self._client, self._base = client, base\n        self.value = get_token(client, base)\n\n    def refresh(self) -> None:\n        self.value = get_token(self._client, self._base)"
}
```

`ping` is too short and hits the 422 validation. I can use that to prove the refresh path without spending any LLM calls — a 422 on the retry means auth passed:

<details><summary>tool_use: Bash — cd /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot uv run python -c " import httpx, script...</summary>

```json
{
  "command": "cd /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot\nuv run python -c \"\nimport httpx, scripts.eval_golden as e\nfrom api.core.config import ApiSettings\nfrom common.config import configure\nconfigure(ApiSettings())\nwith httpx.Client() as c:\n    t = e.PageToken(c, 'http://localhost:8000')\n    stale = 'expired.rubbish.token'\n    t.value = stale\n    try:\n        e.ask(c, 'http://localhost:8000', t, 'ping')   # 422 = auth passed on retry\n    except httpx.HTTPStatusError as err:\n        print('retry status:', err.response.status_code, '(401 would mean the refresh failed)')\n    print('token re-minted:', t.value != stale)\n\" 2>&1 | grep -v Warning",
  "description": "Prove the 401 retry re-mints, without LLM calls",
  "timeout": 120000
}
```

**Result**
```
  page token expired, minting a fresh one
retry status: 422 (401 would mean the refresh failed)
token re-minted: True
```
</details>

Fix verified with zero LLM calls — stale token → 401 → re-mint → retry authenticates.

The run **did not complete** — it crashed at question 42 of 51 and no report files were written. Here's what it did establish, and what it cost.

## Why it crashed

The harness mints one page token per run; `JWT_TTL_SECONDS` is 1800. Retrieval plus 41 answers took longer than 30 minutes, so `/api/chat` started returning 401 and the traceback killed the process — losing every in-memory result. `logs/common/golden_qa*.md` still hold the old runs.

Fixed: `PageToken` re-mints and retries once on a 401. Verified with zero LLM calls by handing it a stale token — 401 → refresh → retry authenticated (422 from a too-short message, not 401).

## Retrieval — complete, and the k=10 change is vindicated

The retrieval pass finished all 42 scoreable questions before the crash:

| | recall | MRR |
|---|---|---|
| set 0 | 18/18 | 0.915 |
| set 1 | 24/24 | 0.763 |
| **both** | **42/42 @ k=10** | 0.828 |

No misses anywhere. The decisive evidence: **three questions retrieve only at ranks 7, 8 and 9** — `qa-011` (the one that used to flip between hit and miss at the k=5 boundary) and `qb-014`/`qb-015` (the two conditional failures the change was made for). All three were unreachable before.

Don't read 0.828 against the old 0.931 — that figure was set 0 alone. Like-for-like, set 0 went 0.931 → 0.915, which is rank noise from a non-deterministic rewrite.

## Answers — 41 of 51 judged

- **Set 0 corpus: 20/21**, down from 21/21
- **Set 0 later batch: 3/5** — `qa-101`/`qa-105`, the known-correct behaviour
- **Set 1: 14/15 of those reached**, including **`qb-015` now passing** (it failed at k=5). `qb-014` still fails despite retrieval finding all three documents at rank 8 — so that one is a synthesis limit, not coverage. `qb-016`–`qb-025` never ran.

The one real regression is **`qa-016`, refused as `harmful_content`** — a benign German question about a device showing as unreachable, with the right chunk at rank 1. This is a different bug from the invented-category one we fixed: a legitimate category applied wrongly, which the closed-set fix can't catch. It passed on every earlier run, so it's intermittent. Logged in `docs/considerations.md` → Improvements as open, and as a watch item in `work.md`, since a false accusation is worse than a wrong answer.

256 tests pass.

**A complete run needs another ~200 calls** (Haiku analysis + generation, Opus 5 judging). I'm not spending that without your say-so. Options: full re-run for clean numbers in both reports, or `--golden 1` only (~90 calls) to finish the set that got cut off.
