Full run completed — the token refresh fired once, exactly as intended, and both reports were written.

## Scores

| | result |
|---|---|
| **Answerable** | **42/42** |
| Must decline | 3/4 unanswerable + 2/5 not-yet-ingested = **5/9** |
| Corpus total | 45/46 |
| Retrieval @k=10 | 41/42, MRR 0.826 |
| Latency | median 3.4 s, **p95 4.97 s**, slowest 5.9 s |
| Per stage | analysis 1.27 s · retrieval 0.56 s · generation 1.38 s · TTFT 2.68 s |

## The changes worked

Every answerable question in both sets is now correct. Specifically:

- **`qb-014` and `qb-015` both pass** — the two conditional failures the k=10 change was made for. `qb-014` retrieved at rank 7, unreachable at k=5.
- **`qa-001` no longer reads the neighbouring table row** — the markdown serialisation fix holding.
- **`qa-016` answered correctly**, so the `harmful_content` refusal was intermittent, not a standing defect. Marked as such.
- Median input tokens 2 100 → 2 747, and p95 latency stayed under the 5 s target.

## The real finding: declining got worse

Four must-decline questions were answered, each with a **fabricated specific lifted from adjacent context**:

- `qb-025` — priced express delivery to Belgium at €59, which is the free-shipping *threshold*, not a price
- `qa-103` — invented "under 1 second" response time for a maintenance tier, from an unrelated sensor spec
- `qa-101`, `qa-105` — the previously-known retention-policy and automation-limit cases

`qa-103` and `qb-025` are new since k=5. I'd read that as the cost of the same change that fixed the conditionals: twice the context means twice the plausible-but-wrong material, and nothing checks that a chunk *answers* the question rather than resembling it. Not a regression to undo — answerable went 40/42 → 42/42 — but declining is now clearly the weakest axis at 5/9, and it's the axis where being wrong is most expensive.

Logged as a new open entry in `docs/considerations.md` → Improvements, with the two levers: a relevance check between retrieval and generation (that's Known issues #3, re-ranking, which now has a second measured trigger), or a prompt that demands the answer be *stated* rather than derivable.

One retrieval note: `qb-015` scored a MISS this run despite answering correctly — it got 2 of its 3 required documents, and the strict multi-hop rule needs all three. The metric is stricter than the task.

Docs updated: `work.md` (re-run closed, two new items), `considerations.md` (k=5 entry now *fixed* with verified results; new over-answering entry; refusal entry marked intermittent), `design-decisions.md` (fresh measurement block replacing the stale k=5 one).

{

  "old_string": "### 3. No re-ranking\n\nFusion output goes straight to the model. On 201 chunks with distinct topics\nRRF is sufficient \u2014 measured MRR 0.931. It stops being sufficient at a corpus\nsize where many chunks are plausibly relevant and near-duplicates crowd the cut;\na cross-encoder over the top ~50 is the answer then, at the cost of a second\nmodel in the request path.",
  "new_string": "### 3. No re-ranking\n\nFusion output goes straight to the model. Nothing checks that a chunk *answers*\nthe question rather than resembling it, which is precisely what over-answering\nis made of (#10) \u2014 so this is no longer a corpus-size argument, it has a\nmeasured trigger.\n\n**Five relevant chunks beat ten that might include misses.** Raising\n`retrieval_top_k` to 10 bought answerable coverage and paid for it in\nfabrication: every extra chunk is more plausible-but-wrong material to assemble\nfrom. A cross-encoder over the top ~50 lets the cut go back down to ~5 while\nkeeping the recall, which is the trade the other way round.\n\nWhat it costs, in order:\n\n- **Compute shape changes.** Cost moves off LLM calls and onto re-ranking \u2014 a\n  second model in the request path, one that scores every candidate rather than\n  embedding one query.\n- **Which argues for splitting model inference into its own service.** Two\n  models in the api container already make it the fat unit; a third makes\n  scaling the request path mean scaling GPU-shaped work with it. Separate\n  services let retrieval, re-ranking and the web tier scale on their own curves\n  \u2014 the same argument as the embedder service, but with enough weight behind it\n  to be worth the split.\n- **Latency may break the 5 s target.** Re-ranking 50 candidates is not free,\n  and total p95 is already 4.97 s.\n- **Then the query-rewrite call is what to reclaim it from.** Analysis is 1.27 s\n  median, the single largest stage after generation, and it is one LLM call on\n  the critical path of every request. Dropping or shrinking it \u2014 a smaller\n  model, or rewriting only when the question looks context-dependent \u2014 is where\n  the budget for re-ranking comes from."
}

No linting, type checking, CI or coverage\n\nRuff, mypy, pre-commit, CI and coverage reporting are all absent \u2014 they buy\nconsistency and safe collaboration rather than working software, which is why\nthey lost to features on a timed solo PoC and would not on a team.\n\n### 10. Declining is weaker than answering\n\nAnswerable questions score **42/42**; must-decline questions score **5/9**. Four\nwere answered with a fabricated specific lifted from adjacent context: `qb-025`\npriced express delivery to Belgium at \u20ac59 (the free-shipping *threshold*, not a\nprice), `qa-103` invented a \"under 1 second\" response time for a maintenance\ntier, `qa-101` a retention policy, `qa-105` an automation limit.\n\nThe number is always sitting next to the question in the context. Raising\n`retrieval_top_k` to 10 doubled how much of that there is \u2014 the same change that\ntook answerable from 40/42 to 42/42, so it is the price of coverage rather than\na regression to undo.\n\nBeing wrong costs most on this axis: a confident fabricated figure is worse than\na wrong answer to an answerable question, because nothing in the reply signals\ndoubt. The fix is re-ranking (#3); the cheaper half-measure is a system prompt\ndemanding the answer be *stated* in the documents rather than derivable from\nthem.\n\n### 11. Safety refusals are non-deterministic\n\n`qa-016`, a benign German troubleshooting question, was refused as\n`harmful_content` on one run and answered correctly on the next, with no change\nto the system. Retrieval had the right chunk at rank 1 both times; the\nclassifier never let it through on the first.\n\nDistinct from the invented-category bug that was fixed \u2014 this is a real category\napplied to a benign question, which a closed set cannot prevent. A false refusal\naccuses the user of something, so it is the worse failure mode.\n\nIntermittent, so the metric is the refusal rate over runs, not the case. Levers:\na lower-variance model for the safety verdict, few-shot examples of\nbenign-but-alarming phrasing, or splitting the verdict off the rewrite call so\neach prompt does one job \u2014 which the latency work in #3 may force anyway."
}
```

Moved. Known issues is now eleven, and the argument chain you laid out is recorded as the substance of #3.

**#3 No re-ranking** — rewritten. It no longer argues from corpus size; it argues from measurement:
- Five relevant chunks beat ten that might include misses — k=10 bought coverage and paid in fabrication, a cross-encoder lets the cut go back to ~5 while keeping recall
- Cost moves off LLM calls onto re-rank compute
- Which argues for splitting model inference into its own service — two models already make the api the fat unit, and a third means scaling the web tier drags GPU-shaped work with it
- Latency may break the 5 s target (p95 is already 4.97 s)
- Then the query-rewrite call is what to reclaim it from — analysis is 1.27 s median, the largest stage after generation, one LLM call on every request's critical path

**#10 Declining is weaker than answering** — 42/42 answerable vs 5/9 must-decline, the four fabrications with their specifics, and the point that the number is always adjacent in context. Notes this is the price of coverage, not a regression, and that a confident fabricated figure is the worse failure because nothing in the reply signals doubt.

**#11 Safety refusals are non-deterministic** — `qa-016` refused then answered with no system change; distinct from the invented-category bug since a closed set can't prevent a real category applied wrongly.

Both are out of Improvements, which keeps its "measured failures and their fixes" meaning. Two consequential edits while consolidating:

- The old `qa-101` Improvements entry duplicated #10 and its closing line had gone stale — it said "the other four not-yet-ingested questions declined correctly", which is now two, not four. Replaced with a three-line pointer to #10, keeping the K564–K568 detail as the first instance.
- The Deferred re-ranking closure said RRF was sufficient at MRR 0.931 — that reasoning is dead. Now reads "**won't do here**, but no longer for want of evidence… out of scope for a timed PoC, not unjustified", pointing at #3 and #10. I kept it closed rather than reopening, since you didn't ask for that; say the word if it should go back to outstanding.

`design-decisions.md`'s re-ranking row updated to match.
